## 2026-10-04 — exact TU2 guest capture and fresh analysis

An isolated research Xenia build now captures the post-title-update guest image after `FinishLoadingUserModule`, without modifying the user-owned XEX, title update, DLC, or Xenia installation. The run logged TU2 application from version `0.0.0.3` to `0.0.2.3` and wrote a 0x01360000-byte image at `0x82000000`, SHA-256 `EC2C955DB9E55A6207A391A2AB80F60332DC41C3FE4C07968C7F07D5A9621CA4`. A fresh Ghidra project imported/analyzed that image and exported 25,470 functions. XenonAnalyse runs successfully but XenonRecomp stalls with zero output against the raw loaded-memory image; the next native repair is a traceable conversion or exact patched XEX/ELF serialization. Native recompilation and runtime bootstrap remain blocked; the pivot Xenia/SBS path remains separate.

### Exact TU2 native translation gate — 2026-10-04

The captured image was converted into a section-preserving big-endian PowerPC ELF with executable `.text`, `.pdata`, data, TLS, embedded, import/export, and relocation sections. XenonRecomp then generated 300 files (209,555,542 bytes) from that exact-image ELF. A Clang 22.1.8 isolated build compiled all generated translation units, linked `fnc_generated.lib`, and linked the native ABI probe. This closes the exact-image generation/compile subgate. The probe still only reports generated metadata and mappings; it does not provide process bootstrap, real service bindings, title initialization, or playable-game evidence. Card 07 therefore remains IN_PROGRESS at runtime bootstrap.

### Exact TU2 generated-config correction — 2026-10-04

The first exact-image generated config contained invalid image/code range constants despite successful translation. The private research config was corrected from the captured PE section map and the entire 300-unit Clang build was rerun. The corrected probe now reports `image_base=0x82000000`, `image_size=0x1360000`, `code_base=0x82290000`, `code_size=0xc545a0`, and the first mapping `0x82290070`. This closes the generated-image metadata subgate only; no game entry, title screen, or runtime service acceptance has been demonstrated.

### Exact TU2 boot probe critic — 2026-10-04

The corrected exact-image probe mounts the captured TU2 PE and installs 75,636 mappings and 302 import slots, then returns from the patched entry after two demand-page commits without reaching title initialization. A fresh scan of the exact generated directory finds 256,572 `// ERROR` markers, including unresolved entry-path calls. Native runtime bootstrap is therefore still blocked on complete call-target coverage and service dispatch; the successful compile/link is retained only as a translation artifact.

### Exact-TU2 repair audit (2026-10-04)

Two evidence-backed XenonRecomp repair cycles were attempted against the exact TU2 guest image. Adding all 64,777 unresolved `.text` targets stalled with zero output; limiting the config to 295 unresolved targets in the entry translation unit also stalled with zero output. Both processes were terminated and no promoted output was changed. The fresh critic therefore keeps the native runtime gate blocked: entry translation still contains unresolved targets such as `0x8229EAF0`, `0x8229F0F0`, and `0x82294EC8`. The next repair must use valid Ghidra function boundaries from the exact TU2 program, not 4-byte branch targets.

A third exact-TU2 repair cycle used fresh Ghidra function boundaries (`0x8229EAF0` size `0x488`, `0x8229F0F0` size `0x968`) rather than branch targets. XenonRecomp still stalled with zero output. The original 300-unit compilation remains the only promoted generated output; native runtime is blocked on translator expansion and unresolved entry calls.

Repair cycle 23 fixed the TOML separator and retried two exact Ghidra function ranges. XenonRecomp still stalled with zero output, so the native gate remains blocked by translator control-flow expansion/overlap handling rather than simple config syntax.

The recompiler “stall” was caused by invalid repair TOML containing a literal newline escape token. Valid serialization restored XenonRecomp completion. ## 2026-10-04 — Seeded full-function native probe baseline
The exact TU2 full-function generated build now compiles and links with zero XenonRecomp unsupported-opcode warnings. The seeded ABI probe mounts the exact guest image, installs 71,137 function mappings and 302 imports, commits six demand pages, and returns successfully. This is a bootstrap baseline only: title initialization has not been reached; 183,007 generated control-flow markers and missing runtime services remain. See `research/generated/EC2C955DB9E55A6207A391A2AB80F60332DC41C3FE4C07968C7F07D5A9621CA4/tu2-native-boot-probe-instructions7-seeded.log`.

### Shared interior-entry audit
The first post-entry control-flow defect is a call to `0x822A0710`, which lies inside the generated function range beginning at `0x822A0358`. A targeted config split was attempted and rejected after XenonRecomp hung without output; no output was adopted. The passing instructions7 build remains the baseline while shared interior-entry lowering is designed and tested.

### Interior-wrapper repair cycle 24
Adding an overlapping generated entry range for `0x822A0710` also hangs XenonRecomp without output and was discarded. The canonical instructions7 build and seeded probe remain unchanged.

### Unresolved-target audit
An independent scan found 183,007 generated markers representing 50,602 unique target addresses. At least 192 targets are interior addresses inside other configured functions. ## FPS timing investigation: static 347 and 349 review — 2026-10-17

Root-recorded static reviews cover FPS347 (53 words across three targets: `sub_82C66B40`/7, `sub_82C77850`/10, `sub_82C71598`/36) and FPS349 (120 words across `sub_827C8E58`/31, `sub_827C90F0`/33, `sub_827C9378`/56). The reviews include raw decompiler C (Ghidra). FPS347 artifacts passed target, pin, rollback, release, and artifact review; its launcher receipt failed to capture the process exit, so that exit remains unobserved. FPS349 completed with exit 0 and passed target, pin, rollback, release, and artifact review. These are static findings; they do not establish in-game FPS behavior.

FPS347's reviewed counter reads timebase64; its converter constructs binary64 and represents 49,875,000 exactly. Its wait path maps thunk `0x82EE369C` to `KeDelayExecutionThread` and thunk `0x82EE351C` to `NtYieldExecution`. For finite 32-bit millisecond values, the wait conversion is `-(ms * 10000)` in signed 64-bit arithmetic; input `0xFFFFFFFF` separately maps to the `MIN64` sentinel. FPS349's setter stores rate at +64 and period at +72 using `fctidz(f32(f32(1e9/rate)+0.5f))`, with single-precision divide and add. Mode 2 clamp was not selected, so no live clamp bug is claimed. ABI uncertainty, the bogus `savegprlr` object-return interpretation, and the raw-decompiler-C f1 return hazard remain open.

Independent benchmark v3 completed all children with exit 0.
